惟可能因市場變化而變動，投資標的之價格與收益將隨時變動，亦不必然為未來績效表現 4

市場焦點
美國
• 9月ISM服務業PMI降至54.9，雖商業活動與新訂單放緩 ，但就業回升至 50.1、訂單積壓增加 ，
顯示服務需求仍具韌性；另一方面，價格指數升至74.0，創2022年7月以來新高，供應商交貨
速度亦放緩 ，反映燃料 、原物料及供應鏈成本升高 ，景氣未明顯轉弱之際 ，通膨壓力反而重
新升溫；同時全球長債殖利率同步走高 ，AI基建投資延續企業成長動能 ，也提高資本需求 。
整體而言 ，美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但通膨與長端利率壓力同步升高 ，使金融條件維持偏緊 。
中國
• 彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與 AI普及下，中國產業快速發展出現供給過剩 ，產業獲利
能力面臨挑戰，未來三年中國AI產業將可能處於虧損狀態，不過即使模型價格下降，AI使用量
增加將帶動Token需求大幅成長，其中具有企業客戶、專有資料及生態優勢的AI龍頭，將比單

純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
黃金
• 歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵 ，加上長期通膨風險升溫 ，宏觀不確定性讓實體黃金
市場逆勢吸金 。本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易 ，實體黃金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中
期的支撐。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以投資組
合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。

美國9月ISM服務需求略降、成本壓力升高，景氣韌性與通膨風險並存
• 服務業維持擴張，需求略降但就業回穩：9月ISM服務業指數降至54.9(前月55.4)，連續27個月位於擴張區間；商業活動
指數降至56.5、新訂單降至 59.8，顯示服務需求較前月放緩 ，但就業指數回升至 50.1、重返擴張區間 ，訂單積壓升至
56.6，顯示內需仍具韌性，服務業尚未出現明顯轉弱訊號。
• 成本與供應鏈壓力升高 ，通膨降溫面臨干擾： 價格指數升至 74.0，創2022年7月以來新高 ，且為7個月來第6次高於70；
供應商交貨指數升至53.2，顯示交貨速度放緩，燃料、柴油、鋼材、銅及記憶體等成本上升，加上供應受限，使服務業
投入成本壓力擴大，通膨降溫進程面臨干擾。
• 市場觀察：美國服務業為經濟成長的重要支撐，目前經濟呈現「需求仍強、通膨升溫」的拉鋸格局，雖服務業景氣支撐

美國企業獲利，但成本上升可能推升利率波動，投資上宜偏向美國短中天期投資級債，兼顧較高收益率與利率風險控管；
股票則可聚焦具定價能力、獲利與現金流穩健的優質企業。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6 資料來源：ISM Services PMI Report， 2026.10.6
ISM服務業指數分項指標變動對比表ISM服務業PMI主要分項指數變化

全球利率同步走高、AI投資持續擴張，長端美債壓力仍高
• 全球長債殖利率同步走高，反映利率環境重新定價：美國財政部長貝森特指出，近期美債殖利率攀升與德國、日本等主
要債市同步上行，反映全球資本成本重新定價，而非市場對美國資產失去信心；加上目前尚未出現資金大規模撤離美債
並轉向其他主權債券的現象，因此債市波動較偏向全球利率環境調整，而非金融系統性風險。
• AI投資增加資金需求，支撐較高利率環境：貝森特亦駁斥AI泡沫論，認為微軟、Alphabet及Meta等大型科技企業仍以
實際獲利與現金流支撐持續擴大AI資本支出，不同於過去缺乏基本面的科技泡沫；龐大的資料中心 、電力與半導體投資
需求提高整體資金成本，也成為推升長天期利率的重要因素之一。
• 市場觀察：目前市場經濟近況屬於利率高檔、成長尚穩的情境中，因而投資策略建議配置大型科技股、AI基建供應鏈及

受惠利差環境的金融股具相對優勢；債券配置則以中短天期投資級債優先，避免長天期債券受利率上行壓力侵蝕。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6
長端殖利率維持今年以來高檔
資料來源：兆豐銀行整理， 2026.10.6

顧
• 美國8月貿易逆差擴大至2025年初以來最高水平：美國提高進口關稅後，貿易失衡問題仍未明顯改善，商務部數據顯示，8
月貿易逆差擴大13.7%至1,056億美元，且超出市場預期的1,021億美元。經通膨調整後，8月商品貿易逆差擴大至1,147億美
元，同樣為2025年3月以來最大。
• 觀察美國本土投資能否進一步轉化為出口能力：AI建設帶動半導體與設備進口，加上能源成本上升及關稅政策干擾，推升進
口額至歷史新高。不過，強勁內需也顯示消費與企業投資仍具支撐力。隨著第三季財報季即將展開，根據Factset數據，分析
師預期第三季標普500企業獲利年增約29.5%，加上企業獲利預期持續上修，強化投資人對高利率環境下企業基本面的信心。
投資策略上，相較於單筆投資，此時的高成長與高波動的環境，更適合分批布局的定期定額策略來參與市場。

美國8月貿易逆差擴大至2025年初以來最高水平
美國進口創下解放日以來的最高水平
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.6

顧
• 實質薪資持續成長，內需復甦動能增強：日本8月實質現金收入年增1.5%，連續第8個月成長，名目工資與基本工資均年增
3.8%，顯示薪資成長已逐步抵銷通膨對購買力的侵蝕；剔除獎金、加班費及抽樣偏差後，全職勞工薪資仍成長2.8%，反映
薪資上漲具有一定持續性。實質所得改善有助家庭消費與內需回升，進一步支撐企業營收與獲利展望。
• 薪資與通膨並行，日股中長期仍具配置價值：薪資持續上升有助日本形成薪資增加、進而消費擴大，導向企業營收改善的正
向循環，同時也強化日本央行持續推進貨幣政策正常化的條件。雖然升息可能推升日圓與資金成本，但金融股可受惠於利差
擴大，且企業治理改革、AI投資及自動化需求仍提供獲利支撐。近期日股AI與半導體族群已明顯走強，顯示市場仍重視企業
獲利成長；中長期建議維持日股正向看法，聚焦金融、AI、機器人、自動化及電力設備等具結構性成長題材的企業。

顧
中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境
• 中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境 ：彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與
AI普及下，中國AI產業快速發展 ，但也逐漸出現供給過剩 ，使產業獲
利能力面臨挑戰 。開源模式雖有助於降低技術門檻 、應用普及 ，但也
使技術優勢難以長期維持 ，加上中國市場長期習慣低價甚至免費的數
位服務，多數AI業者難以透過訂閱模式獲利，轉而依賴利潤較低的API
服務作為主要收入來源 。目前中國已備案大型語言模型 (LLM)數量達
988個，包括DeepSeek、Kimi及智譜AI等新創企業快速崛起，進一步
加劇市場競爭。此外API服務逐漸被視為基礎公用服務，即使AI Agent
帶動詞元(Token)需求快速成長，也未必能同步提升獲利，由於開源模
型可由不同雲端業者部署 ，市場進入門檻降低 ，業者更難建立長期競

爭優勢。
• 後市展望： 考量龐大資本支出與持續價格競爭 ，未來三年中國 AI產業
整體仍可能處於虧損狀態 ，市場將形成「美國閉源、高毛利」與「中
國開源 、低成本 」模式 ，即使模型價格下降 ，AI 使用量增加將帶動
Token需求大幅成長 ，進而推升資料中心 、雲端服務及 AI晶片需求 ，
從投資角度來看，基礎設施供應商(晶片、伺服器、資料中心、雲端)受
惠程度可能高於模型開發商；而具有企業客戶 、專有資料及生態優勢
的AI龍頭，將比單純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
中國AI市場規模預測
中國已備案的LLM數量

顧
人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台，有望提振中國經濟
和市場信心
• 人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台 ，有望提振中國經濟和市場信心 ：日前中國推出購房貸款貼息政策及下調
PSL(抵押補充貸款)利率，代表穩增長政策進一步加碼 ，有助於提振市場信心與經濟預期 ，此次政策不僅支持房地產市場 ，也
將「六張網」建設、科技創新、民營及小微企業 ，納入重點扶持範圍 ，透過增加再貸款額度 ，與提高支持比例 ，帶動投資、
就業及消費回升。短期A股有望在政策推動下出現築底反彈，其中基建投資、製造業投資、房市銷售及居民消費有機會改善，
同時PSL(抵押補充貸款)降息有助於流動性環境邊際寬鬆 ，降低市場對經濟增長的疑慮 ，後市來看企業獲利與信用環境有望逐
步修復，進一步鞏固A股慢牛格局。投資建議上房貸貼息政策有利房地產、建材、家居等產業鏈，金融業受惠資金成本下降與

信貸回升，食品飲料、零售、家電及服務消費則可望受惠於居民消費意願提升。
資料來源：華金證券，2026.10.5

中國、日本減持美國公債的背後邏輯與差異
• 橋水基金創始人達利歐警告 ，美國公債正面臨中日兩國減持的風險 ，數據顯示這項警告已成事實 。2026年前七個月，中國
美國公債持倉減少660億美元至6,180億美元；同期日本持倉亦減少820億美元至1.1兆美元，扣除價格變動的估值效應後，
兩國實質持倉均大幅下滑。然而，兩國削減美國公債的背後動機並不相同，這將導致未來的減持軌跡出現明顯分化。
• 中國戰略性長期去美元化：1.降低地緣政治風險與轉向資產多元化 ，隨著人民幣在國際貿易結算中使用增加 ，市場流動性提
升，中國對美元儲備依賴度顯著下降。2.美債財政可持續性隱憂，美國政府債務持續攀升，引發對美國長期財政健康疑慮。
3.根據彭博經濟研究模型預測，在漸進式減持情境下，中國的美元儲備(以美債與機構債為主)到2050年可能減少約50%。

• 中國搭配戰術性節奏調整 (短期緩和因素)：儘管長期趨勢向下 ，但目前中美利差顯著 (美中30年期公債利差超過 350個基點)，
在美債殖利率維持高位期間，過快減持意味著實現債券價格下跌的虧損，且可能加劇人民幣升值壓力。因此，中國短中期內
傾向採取「戰略性減持、戰術性放緩」的靈活步調。

日本受政策利率與本土資本回流驅動
• 與中國的結構性考量不同，日本的減持主要出自週期性因素與匯率干預需求。
• 日本央行貨幣政策轉鷹： 日本央行正加快升息腳步 。隨著日債殖利率上揚 (30年期日債殖利率升至約 4.2%)，美日利差持續
收窄，吸引日本國內機構資金回流本土主權債市場。
• 匯率干預政策：日本為防範日圓過度貶值，日本財務省傾向出售部分海外美債資產以籌集外匯干預資金。若日圓再次面臨壓
力，此類拋售干預可能持續。因此，隨著日本央行逐步走向政策正常化，日本對美債的持倉大概會延續下滑軌跡。
• 小結與投資建議：美國公債作為全球無風險資產的地位正面臨結構性挑戰，中國的「長期去美元化」與日本的「本土利率回
升資金回流」將形成雙重壓力，這意味著美債殖利率短期內難大幅下行，美國財政部發債成本居高不下，進而推高全球資本

的融資成本。投資債券配置建議採取「中短天期＋階梯式配置」策略，長天期美國公債(20年以上)受發債供給過剩及中日兩
國長期減持影響，價格波動較大。建議投資人適度降低長天期美國公債暴險，轉向防禦性較佳的中短天期美國公債或高品質
高評級公司債，以鎖定當前較高殖利率同時控管利率風險。
殖利率差：美元與人民幣 vs 美元與日圓

黃金市場資金流向分歧，實體投資有望支撐中長期金價表現
• 利率與匯率因素，壓抑金價近期表現：9月以來，美國公債殖利率飆升、美元指數走揚，由於黃金本身不孳息，資金傾向轉
往固定收益市場，疊加美元走強壓抑國際購買意願，金價自8月底高點至今已下修約11%。
• 9月資金流向分歧，實體投資強、期貨市場弱：根據世界黃金協會統計，9月在金價下跌之際，全球實體黃金ETF卻吸引大量
資金流入，流入約100億美元(67噸)。另一方面，COMEX黃金期貨市場投機部位大幅下降 ，避險基金與其他機構投資人的
多頭部位明顯減少，造成期貨市場賣壓增加，進一步推動金價下跌。
• 實體黃金市場逆勢吸金反應投資人的避險需求：歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵，加上美伊衝突未解及AI算力需求強
勁，長期通膨風險升溫，宏觀的不確定性讓實體黃金市場逆勢吸金。從數據上來看，本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易，實體黃

金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中期的支撐 。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以
投資組合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，2026.10.7。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月通膨數據高於市場預期，CPI 月增 0.6%、年增 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增
0.3%、年增 2.6%。同月 PPI 月增 1.3%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最大增幅，年增
6.3%， 顯示通膨壓力已逐步由能源價格擴散至更廣泛領域 。 數據公布後 ， 市場一
度提高對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，但隨後多位官員釋出審慎訊號，使升息押注
降溫 。 聯準會理事鮑曼表示 ， 決策前仍有時間觀察先前升息效果 ； 舊金山聯準銀
行總裁戴莉則認為 10 月無立即升息之必要。而褐皮書顯示，美國就業市場維持
「低招聘、低裁員」格局，而高能源價格帶來的通膨壓力仍值得關注。
⚫ 中國 9 月通膨持續回升，CPI 年增 0.9%，創近三個月高點；PPI 年增 5.0%，創
逾四年最大漲幅，顯示通縮壓力進一步減輕。主要受能源與原物料價格上漲帶
動，且 9 月進出口表現優於預期，AI 產業鏈需求與貿易環境改善提供支撐；不

過，信貸增長仍仰賴政府發債與季底效應，內需與民間融資動能仍偏弱。
⚫ 日本銀行審議委員小枝淳子表示 ， 不宜對升息過於保守 ， 並認為政策利率仍有進
一步上調空間。總裁植田和男則強調維持2%通膨目標的重要性。整體而言，日
本央行持續釋出偏鷹派訊號，有助支撐日圓表現。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委溫施表示 ， 在能源價格波動下 ，為了控制通膨仍不排除進一步升息
可能，總裁拉加德則認為法國債務問題應透過財政政策解決， 歐洲央行官員普遍
認為能源價格的潛在波動依然是推升通膨並使其居高不下的主要風險來源。。
⚫ 澳洲央行會議紀錄指出，能源價格上漲與需求韌性可能使通膨更具黏性，保留
進一步升息空間。然而 9 月就業數據意外疲弱，市場大幅下調升息預期，目前
預估 11 月維持利率不變的機率接近 90%。

Document 041: 2026年10月LPR报价保持不变，短期内下调可能性较小

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月LPR报价保持不变，短期内下调可能性较小.pdf
Report source: 东方金诚
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

东方金诚宏观研究

1 
2026 年 10 月 LPR 报价保持不变，短期内下调可能性较小 
 王青  冯琳 
2026 年 10 月 20 日，中国人民银行授权全国银行间同业拆借中心公布新版 LPR 报价：1
年期品种报 3.0%，上月为 3.0%；5 年期以上品种报 3.5%，上月为 3.5%。 
解读：

10 月两个期限品种的 LPR 报价保持不变，符合市场预期。主要是 9 月末央行调整完善若
干货币政策工具，主要政策利率保持稳定，这意味着 10 月 LPR 报价的定价基础没有变化。另
外，当前银行净息差仍然处在历史低位附近，报价行也缺乏主动下调 LPR 报价加点的动力。 
我们认为，近期 LPR 报价保持稳定，根本原因是三季度 GDP 增速回升到 4.5%，以高技术
制造业为代表的新质生产力领域加速发展。在此背景下，总量型货币政策保持定力，货币政
策加大逆周期调节力度优先选择结构性货币政策工具发力。 
展望未来，短期内 LPR 报价下调的可能性不大。主要原因在于，9 月 29 日央行调整完善
若干结构性货币政策工具，包括 PSL 利率下调 0.25 个百分点，支农支小再贷款额度上调
5000 亿，其中民企再贷款额度上调 3000 亿，科技创新和技术改造再贷款额度上调 2000 亿，
“报销”比例由原来的 60%上调至 100%；与此同时，财政部门也会同央行、金融监管总局，
首次出台了针对部分首套刚需购房贷款的贴息政策。我们判断，在以上结构性稳增长、稳楼
市措施出台后，接下来将进入一段政策效果观察期。短期内总量型降息政策跟进的可能性下
降，LPR 报价的定价基础会保持稳定。 
再往后看，综合考虑经济金融形势，预计 2027 年央行有望全面降息 10 到 20 个基点
（0.1 到0.2 个百分点），届时将引导 LPR 报价跟进下调。 
10 月 19 日央行宣布，“将研究择机把央行公开市场主要操作品种的期限切换为隔夜，
公开市场隔夜回购利率 将相应成为主要政策利率。”这意味着政策利率“换锚”的大方向已
经确定，未来 LPR 报价的定价基础将由现在的央行 7 天期逆回购利率切换为隔夜逆回购利
率。背后是隔夜政策利率与货币市场隔夜利率（ DR001)之间天然匹配，不存在期限利差，更
有利于提高货币政策传导效率。我们判断，切换时点可能已为时不远，不排除较快落地的可

### Page 2

东方金诚宏观研究

2 
能，届时央行将公开宣布隔夜逆回购操作利率。不过，切换过程带动 LPR 报价下调的可能性
不大；未来 LPR 报价变化，还会主要跟进政策利率水平本身调整。 
10 月 19 日央行还宣布，“推动贷款利率定价基准改革，从比较单一地参考贷款市场报
价利率（LPR）定价向更加多元的定价基准转型。”前期一些省份的银行已经开始发放以 DR
为定价基础的贷款。这意味着贷款利率市场化改革进一步推进，即从原来的单一 LPR 定价基
础，转为“LPR+DR”双锚。LPR 属于机构报价型利率，稳定性较高，而 DR 属于成交型利率，
市场化特征突出。信贷定价转为“ LPR+DR”双锚，意味着后期部分贷款利率市场化定价程度
会更高。这一方面有利于提升货币政策传导效率，加速政策利率向贷款利率传导，另一方面
也能更好满足不同市场主体的融资需求 ——对一些短期流动资金需求大、财务能力强的大型
企业来说，DR 基准贷款更能适配其信贷需求。我们判断，未来 10 年期国债收益率也可能成
为部分贷款的定价基础，背后是一些大型企业有条件在信贷市场和债券市场之间选择融资。
这意味着贷款定价基础多元化还将进一步推进。

市場焦點
美國
• 9月綜合PMI升至58.4，創2021年7月以來新高 ，製造業與服務業同步加速 ，新訂單及就業亦
明顯成長 ，顯示消費 、AI投資與國防支出支撐景氣動能 。不過，需求快速擴張也加劇供應鏈
瓶頸，企業投入價格升至 2022年10月以來高點，燃料、運輸及原物料成本上升 ，顯示景氣韌
性與通膨壓力同步升溫，增加Fed政策維持緊縮的壓力。
美國
期中選舉
• 目前民調顯示 ，民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數 ，這將有助於縮減未來財政赤字預期 ，進
而帶動10年期美債殖利率下滑 ，對長天期美債形成支撐 。此外既有資料顯示 ，選後一年美股
平均報酬率達12.4%，主因為政治不確定性降低，市場焦點重新回到經濟成長、企業獲利及貨
幣政策等基本面因素，進而帶動投資信心提升。

越南
• 越南股市正式納入富時羅素新興市場指數 。估計此次納入最高可引導 60億美元的資金重新流
入越南。資產管理公司Vanguard也計畫在未來幾年內，將對越南的投資額提升至約 25億美元。
• 越南股市短期仍面臨逆風 ，除了能源通膨壓力居高不下恐拖累經濟成長 ，今年以來越南成為
美國最大貿易逆差國，加上美方企圖阻止越南成為中國商品流入的中轉國 ，美對越的301條款
調查為越南出口額帶來更多不確定性。

美國景氣強勢擴張，通膨與利率壓力再升
• 企業活動創逾五年新高 ，製造業與服務業同步加速擴張 ：S&P
Global美國9月綜合PMI初值升至58.4，創2021年7月以來新高，
且連續四個月加速成長；服務業 PMI升至58.7（59個月高點）、
製造業PMI升至57.0（52個月高點），產出亦同步加速 ，加上
訂單與就業成長皆達近年最快 ，顯示內需、AI投資及國防支出
共同支撐企業活動。S&P Global依PMI調查推估經濟年成長約
5%，Q3則上看4%，反映短期景氣動能明顯增強。
• 供給受限推高成本 ，通膨壓力再升溫： 需求快速擴張同時推高
供應鏈與產能壓力 ，企業未完成訂單升至 2022年5月以來高點 ，
供應商交貨延遲亦明顯惡化 ，反映產能不足 ，投入價格指數升

至2022年10月以來新高 ，企業主要反映燃料 、運輸、原物料
及薪資成本上升 ，顯示供給受限與能源成本可能持續向終端價
格傳導，增加通膨黏著風險並提高Fed維持緊縮政策的壓力。
• 後市觀察：數據公布後，10年期美債殖利率一度升至約 5.12%，
創2007年以來新高 ，市場對10月再升息的押注亦升溫 ，短線
需關注供應鏈壓力是否緩解 ，以及後續ISM、就業與物價數據
能否確認通膨再升溫趨勢 。投資策略建議關注利率敏感度較低
的高品質成長股或防禦型資產 ，可適度布局受惠強勁內需與 AI
／國防支出的美股大型科技與工業類股 ，並留意升息環境下金
融股的利差受益機會 ，整體建議維持分散配置並嚴控利率風險 。
資料來源：S&P Global PMI, Bureau of Economic Analysis via S&P Global Market
Intelligence， 2026.9.24

能源成本升高推升通膨，利率維持高檔壓力升溫
• 能源與運輸成本上升，增加通膨黏著風險：中東衝突與
俄羅斯煉油設施遭攻擊 ，使全球柴油供應持續偏緊 ，燃
料價格及運輸成本同步上升；由於柴油廣泛應用於運輸 、
農業與製造業，價格上漲將透過物流及生產成本向下游
傳導。目前全球柴油庫存偏低 、煉油產能受限，若供應
中斷延續，可能使能源價格維持高檔並延長通膨壓力。
• 公債與企業債供給增加 ，長端利率壓力升高：美國財政
赤字及政府融資需求偏高 ，加上大型科技公司持續為 AI
資料中心等基礎建設籌資 ，債券供給增加使市場承接壓
力升高；在通膨預期未完全穩定的情況下 ，投資人對長
天期債券要求較高的期限溢酬 ，進一步推升長端殖利率 ，
同時AI相關企業債近期亦出現利差擴大 ，反映市場對大

量發債及資本支出回收期的關注升高。
• 資產配置面臨成長與通膨雙重拉扯： 能源價格、企業融
資成本及長端殖利率若持續處於高檔 ，將提高企業資金
成本並壓縮高估值資產的評價空間；同時 ，利率與能源
價格波動也可能擴大股 、債市震盪。因此資產配置建議
降低長天期債券對利率變化的敏感度 ，提高對通膨與利
率波動的防禦能力 ，並以現金流穩定、具備定價能力之
優質標的為核心。
資料來源：Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis via FRED ， 2026.9.8

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.9

美國期中選舉-目前民調顯示民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數
• 目前民調顯示民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數 ：最新民調顯示，民主黨
於11月美國期中選舉，取得國會多數席次的機率已領先共和黨，根據市場
情境分析，期中選舉不影響既有法案執行 ，但將左右未來兩年的財政預算 、
稅制改革 、政府支出及國會監督權限 。情境(1)若共和黨續掌國會 ，減稅
政策延長、產業回流、關稅及移民限制等議程有望加速推進 ，財政擴張力
道可能進一步增強 。情境(2)分裂國會 ，兩黨制衡將使重大法案推動難度
提高，新增減稅與大型財政刺激方案較難落地 。情境(3)民主黨全面掌控
國會，則可能提高對政府政策的監督力度 ，政策方向偏向提高稅負與監管 ，
壓抑企業獲利 ，使政策環境趨向平衡。

美國期中選舉-對於股債市的影響
• 對股市的影響：美國銀行分析1900至2025年共31次美國期中選舉，資料發現標普500在期中選舉前12個月平均報酬約為2.9%，
低於所有年度平均 8.9%。因為市場在選舉前往往受到政策不確定性 、政黨競爭及投資人觀望情緒影響 ，波動相對較大 。然而
此現象不代表選舉必然導致市場疲弱，因為當時的經濟環境、通膨、利率及企業獲利等因素同樣扮演重要角色。期中選舉後12
個月的股市表現通常較佳 。歷史數據顯示，選後一年美股平均報酬率達12.4%。主因為政治不確定性降低，市場焦點重新回到
經濟成長、企業獲利及貨幣政策等基本面因素，進而帶動投資信心提升。
• 對債市的影響：若民主黨取得國會主導權，市場普遍預期財政支出擴張幅度將較受節制 ，有助於縮減未來財政赤字預期，進而

帶動10年期美債殖利率下滑，對長天期美債形成支撐，若共和黨持續全面掌控國會，市場可能預期減稅及財政擴張政策延續，
進一步推升財政赤字預估，帶動公債殖利率走高，對長天期美債形成壓力，不過市場已部分反映相關預期，實際衝擊幅度有限。
美國期中選舉前後對於美股的影響
資料來源：美國銀行，2026.7
資料來源：復華投信，2026.9

AI建設熱潮吞噬自由現金流
• 資本支出創新高：雲端服務巨頭(包括亞馬遜、Alphabet、微軟、Meta、甲骨文以及SpaceX等)正在全力擴充AI算力與資料
中心，2026年大型雲端服務巨頭的資本支出合計預估將接近 9,600億美元，而2025至2031年的累計AI資本支出更可能逼近
9兆美元。
• 自由現金流出現赤字： 高額的投資加上股東回報(股息與庫藏股買回)，使許多巨頭的自由現金流(FCF)轉為負值(如Alphabet、
亞馬遜、微軟、Meta及甲骨文等)，預計到2027年，部分企業將更加依賴外部融資，而非單靠內部現金生成。
• 債券發行量大增： 為了填補資金缺口 ，科技業債券發行規模急遽上升 ，2026年迄今，各幣別的投資級科技債券發行量已突
破4,000億美元，創下歷史新高。

顧
獲利韌性與金庫深度：強勁EBITDA成為信用安全網
• 上兆美元的流動性護城河： 儘管債務增加，但科技十二巨頭合計持有近 1兆美元的等值現金，整體科技板塊流動性充裕 ，短
期內無流動性危機。
• EBITDA強勁成長消化債務：市場預期覆蓋的12家最大科技企業在2026與2027年的EBITDA合計將成長約35%，強勁的獲利
能力顯著降低槓桿比率的上升壓力，確保信評機構維護穩定的信用評級。
• 資本配置策略分化：(1)兼顧股東回報：蘋果、微軟、Meta與Alphabet在投入AI同時，仍維持大規模的庫藏股買回與股息發
放。(2)全數投入資本支出：亞馬遜將絕大部分現金導向資本支出(2026年資本支出預計達2,200億美元居首)，且不發放股息。
• 小結與投資建議：科技巨頭為維持AI技術與基礎設施領先優勢，短期內，負自由現金流將成為常態。然而，這並非財務危機

訊號，而是企業利用超強獲利能力 (高EBITDA)與龐大的現金儲備進行戰略性擴張 ，科技板塊的信用風險目前處於可控且健
康的狀態，投資建議聚焦AI變現能力與EBITDA成長，優先佈局具有強大獲利護城河且能將算力轉化為實質營收的龍頭企業 。
科技十二巨頭的等值現金
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.23

越南股市正式納入富時羅素新興市場指數
• 預估引導60億美元資金流入越南： 富時羅素估計，此次納入最高可引導 60億美元的資金重新流入越南 。受此提振，資產管
理公司Vanguard也計畫在未來幾年內，將對越南的投資額提升至約25億美元。整個指數過渡將分為四個階段推行至2027年：
今年9月率先納入10%，明年3月追加20%，最後於明年6月與9月分別完成剩餘各35%的分配。市場分析指出，隨著明年3月
配額加大，市場關注度與對本地投資人的影響將更為顯著。
• 越南股市短期仍面臨逆風： 儘管官方早前誓言今年 GDP成長目標達10%，但中東衝突以來能源通膨壓力居高不下恐拖累經
濟成長。此外，越南與美國的貿易協商仍在進行中，由於今年以來越南成為美國最大貿易逆差國，加上美方企圖阻止越南成

為中國商品流入的中轉國，美對越的301條款調查為越南出口額帶來更多不確定性。
• 觀點：儘管短線面臨不確定性 ，但越南股市同時具備多項利多題材 ，包含企業獲利成長、公共投資加速、FDI回流及資本市
場改革等。中長期結構仍偏正向，可以衛星配置的角度布局，或是以定期定額的方式參與長期成長潛力。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.09。

1. 本資料內容僅供內部同仁及客戶參考，非作為任何金融商品或服務之購買或銷售之推介，不得視為買賣有價證券或其他金融商品之要約或要約之引誘。
2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

社融新增超预期增长，政府债券发行提速 
——2026 年 9 月金融数据点评

陈曦（分析师） 王帅中（联系人）  
chenxi2@kysec.cn 
证书编号：S0790521100002 
wangshuaizhong@kysec.cn 
证书编号：S0790125070016

事件：央行公布 2026 年 9 月金融数据，社融新增 3.55 万亿元，社融存量同比增
长 7.1%，较前值下降 0.1pct；截至 2026 年 9 月社会融资规模增量累计为 27.46
万亿元，同比减少 26290 亿元；M1 同比增长 3.2%，M2 同比增长 7.2%，M0 同
比增长 10.6%，前九个月净投放现金 9221 亿元。 
⚫ 9 月金融数据关注点 
1、9 月社融新增 3.55 万亿元，略低于 2021-2025 年同期均值，同比多增 177 亿
元。Wind 统计的 10 家机构预测中位数为 3.20 万亿元，平均数为 3.13 万亿元，
社融新增超预期增长，政府债净融资高增是社融增长的主要支撑项，人民币贷
款仍为社融的主要拖累。新增人民币贷款较 2025 年同期下降，同比少增 3347
亿元，是社融同比的最大拖累项。政府债券净融资多增 3469 亿元，是社融同比
的最主要支撑，7 月底中央政治局会议中指出宏观政策要发力提效，加快财政支
出和债券资金使用进度后， 政府债发行提速， 后续或将持续成为社融增长的主要
支撑项。企业债券净融资 9 月并未延续正增长，较 2025 年同期少增 529 亿元，
非金融企业境内股票融资较 2025 年同期多增 159 亿元，股票融资的方式仍对银
行贷款形成良性的替代和分流。 
2、贷款“降速提质”或成为宏观运行的新常态，居民端贷款有待修复，企（事）
业单位贷款同比增长主要依靠票据冲量，9 月新增人民币贷款同比下降 16.3%，
增量远低于近 10 年均值水平，但降幅较 8 月有所收窄。居民新增贷款同比少增
3890 亿元，短期、中长期贷款均同比少增，居民端持续去杠杆，短期贷款少增
或仍反映居民端需求偏弱， 对后续收入前景难确认， 消费更加谨慎， 中长期贷款
少增或仍与房地产市场持续低迷有关， 居民端贷款亟需修复。 企业端新增贷款同
比多增 700 亿元，其中票据融资同比多增 3526 亿元，短期、中长期贷款均同比
少增， 新质生产力对银行贷款的需求下降， 地方政府化债对贷款的置换， 以及债
券、 股票等其他融资渠道重要性上升， 都会对贷款读数产生影响， 但中长期贷款
降幅较 8 月有所收窄。 
3、M1、M2 同比均下降， 剪刀差走阔。9 月 M1 同比增长 3.2%， 较前值下降 0.9pct；
M2 同比增长 7.2%，较前值下降 0.3pct，剪刀差绝对值由 3.4%走阔至 4.0%。M1
增速回落或主要受 2025 年同期高基数影响，M2 回落与信贷偏弱和社融读数回
落保持一致。 
4、支撑社融增速见底回升的积极因素正逐渐增多。往后看，7 月底中央政治局
会议中指出宏观政策要发力提效， 加快财政支出和债券资金使用进度后政策陆续
出台加码，9 月底央行进一步调整完善货币政策工具，下调 PSL 利率并扩大 PSL
支持领域， 财政端实施居民购房贷款贴息政策。 待政策落地， 资本金投放后，此
前社融持续下行对经济的影响或将告一段落， 支撑社融增速见底回升的积极因素
正逐渐增多， 若社融增速见底回升， 这将会重新成为经济增长的支撑点和拉动点。 
⚫ 债市观点：在债市“资产荒”充分演绎后，应警惕债市调整风险 
2026 年上半年“资产荒”持续演绎，主要有三大因素驱动，资金利率持续下行、
非银久期处于低位、基本面数据走弱支撑债市走强。 
目前三大利多已经出现边际变化，警惕债市调整风险。 
资金面层面，4 月后资金利率持续回升，6 月以来 DR001 中枢稳定在 1.4%左右，
回归 2025 年下半年水平，市场宽松预期证伪；拥挤度层面，当前公募等非银机
构债券久期已达近年高位，存在抱团瓦解、估值回调风险；基本面层面，6 月起
政策边际加码、 工业数据回暖，消费同比数据也或将随高基数效应消退逐步修复，
基本面由债市利多转为利空。此外， 市场主流的“资产荒支撑债牛”逻辑或不成立：
贷款疲软是长期的新常态，且本轮债市上涨， 由非银抱团买入驱动， 并非银行缺
资产导致的配置行为。 
整体来看，在债市“资产荒”已经充分演绎之后，特别是如果降息预期发酵 /或者
落地，应警惕债市调整风险。 
⚫ 风险提示：政策变化超预期；经济变化超预期。

式实施。根据上述规定，开源证券评定此研报的风险等级为R3（中风险） ，因此通过公共平台推送的研报其适用的
投资者类别仅限定为专业投资者及风险承受能力为C3、C4、C5的普通投资者。若您并非专业投资者及风险承受能
力为C3、C4、C5的普通投资者，请取消阅读，请勿收藏、接收或使用本研报中的任何信息。

表的观点均如实反映分析人员的个人观点。负责准备本报告的分析师获取报酬的评判因素包括研究的质量和准确

股票投资评级说明
评级 说明
证券评级
买入（Buy） 预计相对强于市场表现 20%以上；
增持（outperform） 预计相对强于市场表现 5%～20%；
中性（Neutral） 预计相对市场表现在－5%～＋5%之间波动；
减持（underperform） 预计相对弱于市场表现 5%以下。
行业评级
看好（overweight） 预计行业超越整体市场表现；
中性（Neutral） 预计行业与整体市场表现基本持平；
看淡（underperform） 预计行业弱于整体市场表现。
备注：评级标准为以报告日后的 6~12 个月内，证券相对于市场基准指数的涨跌幅表现，其中 A 股基准指数为沪
深 300 指数、港股基准指数为恒生指数、新三板基准指数为三板成指（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针
对做市转让标的） 、 美股基准指数为标普500 或纳斯达克综合指数。我们在此提醒您， 不同证券研究机构采用不同
的评级术语及评级标准。我们采用的是相对评级体系，表示投资的相对比重建议；投资者买入或者卖出证券的决

定取决于个人的实际情况，比如当前的持仓结构以及其他需要考虑的因素。投资者应阅读整篇报告，以获取比较
完整的观点与信息，不应仅仅依靠投资评级来推断结论。
分析、估值方法的局限性说明
本报告所包含的分析基于各种假设， 不同假设可能导致分析结果出现重大不同。 本报告采用的各种估值方法及模型
均有其局限性，估值结果不保证所涉及证券能够在该价格交易。

### Page 1

Nanya Technology

Upbeat 3Q margin/ASP, and 2027 guidance 
also optimistic: lift PO to NT$800

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 800.00 TWD | Price: 527.00 TWD

3Q results confirm super-cycle even with legacy DRAM 
Nanya Tech, a pure DRAM supplier, reported very strong 3Q results (sales: NT$134bn, 
+62% QoQ / +612% YoY; OPM: 80% - similar to big-3 DRAM makers’; net margin: 66%). 
The key contributor should be upbeat ASP (up 60%+ QoQ vs consensus of 30-40%). In 
fact, Nanya Tech had already reported more than doubled 1H ASP (60%+ QoQ for two 
consecutive quarters). Thus, Street expected limited QoQ price increase in 3Q (vs 1H).  
According to Nanya Tech President Pei Ing Lee, demand for legacy DRAM products 
(DDR3 & DDR4) remained strong across IT, industrial, and enterprise applications. Mgmt 
also said legacy DRAM accounted for 80%+ of 3Q sales (vs 10% range for DDR5). Net-
net, the 3Q results confirmed super-cycle even selling mainly legacy/conventional DRAM. 
Guidance broadly bullish; no sign of a price cut 
Lee provided bullish guidance (earnings call). What we learned: (1) rising 4Q ASP, (2) 
ongoing shortage of legacy DRAM through 2027 (no immediate demand shift from 
DDR4 to DDR5), (3) new fab mass production (2027-28), (4) significant FCF generation 
(with high margin profile) despite large capex spend (NT$70bn in 2026), (5) record-high 
net cash (NT$270bn in 3Q), (6) new node development (in-house) and migration (1c, 1d, 
1e) on track, and (7) new construction plan for 3D wafer packaging/testing facilities in 
Ping Tung Science Park, Taiwan. Overall, new guidance presents further growth (capacity 
expansion) and high margin profile (favorable ASP trend). 
Lift PO to NT$800 on revisions to 2027/28 EPS estimates 
We lift our PO from NT$750 to NT$800 using the same target P/E vs earlier (9x 2027-
28E EPS). Thus, our PO revision is mostly based on 7-8% revisions to 2027-28 EPS 
estimates. We assume 5%/1ppt higher sales/OPM vs our earlier forecasts given more 
favorable conventional DRAM ASP trend. To be conservative, we assume negative EPS 
growth for 2028 but it can turn positive if the ASP remains stable. We reiterate Buy.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Nanya Technology is an affiliate of Formosa Plastics Group. 
The firm manufactures and distributes DRAM chips. 
Management focuses on non-PC applications such as 
consumer, mobile, server, autos, etc. Key customers are 
based in China and Taiwan. Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron 
are major competitors.

Investment Rationale 
Our Buy rating is based on the DRAM industry's supercycle, 
which should warrant a high-margin profile for Nanya Tech 
even in the long term. Its 3Q26 results proved record-high 
revenue (NT$134bn, more than 5x higher than the previous 
peak cycle) and 80% operating margin. Management focus 
on long-term contracts, including DDR4 for eSSD or servers, 
should also act as a catalyst. Significantly rising net cash 
even after capex hike could also support higher dividend 
payouts.

### Page 16

Price objective basis & risk 
Nanya Technology (NNYAF) 
Our PO of NT$800 is derived from 9x 2027-28E P/E, which is c.30% below the long-
term historical average (13x during 2014-25), but near-50% above the previous upturn 
period (6x in 2014-18). Memory stocks usually trade at low valuation multiples during 
peak cycle due to downturn concerns. However, the current DRAM cycle looks 
structurally much better than ever and we expect 2027-28 earnings to exceed 2026 level 
due to the AI growth theme. This could lead to a re-rating (e.g., 9x P/E or higher), with 
solid earnings outlook.

Upside risks: DRAM price increases even after the 1H26 upturn, Big-3 memory 
chipmakers' significant supply cut for commodity DRAM due to HBM, and Nanya Tech's 
more successful ramp-up of DDR4 or DDR5 (better cost or margin improvement).

Downside risks: weaker legacy DDR4 demand (quicker shift to DDR5), and China's more 
successful memory production increase.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings ar e: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

Stronger revenue momentum while GM guidance came in softer 
TSMC hosted its 3Q26 analyst meeting on October 15. While we 
view the higher full-year 2026 revenue guidance and the further 
acceleration in capacity buildout as the key positives, the GM 
guidance could be the softer spot. In the meeting, management 
further raised its 2026 revenue growth outlook to grow by around 
44% YoY (from slightly above 40%; USD term), supported by 
continued strong demand especially for AI/HPC. However, both 
3Q26 GM result and 4Q26 GM guidance came in lower than our 
expectations; we believe this is to reflect a steeper N2 ramp and 
overseas fab dilution in the near term. 
AI demand still ahead of supply, with no sign of slowdown 
Key questions among investors largely surrounded demand outlook. 
Overall, we think that management’s tone on AI demand remained 
very bullish. While TSMC did not formally update its long-term AI 
accelerator revenue CAGR, management indicated the CAGR will 
probably be higher, noting demand is extremely robust with no sign 
of slowdown and still far exceeds supply, with multiple drivers 
reinforcing this view, including - 1) agentic AI driving incremental 
CPU demand, which is excluded from TSMC’s AI accelerator 
definition and implies upside beyond the headline CAGR; 2) broader 
adoption of open-source models lowering the barrier to AI usage 
and lifting leading-edge silicon demand; 3) continued demand for 
networking chips alongside AI accelerators; and 4) TSMC’s own 
checks on US data center build progress and power supply, where 
management sees CSPs as very well prepared.  
Capacity build-out accelerating again: N2 capacity more than 
double in 2027; additional N3 fab addition; Texas under evaluation 
On N2, management for the first time guided its N2 capacity will be

### Page 3

more than double in 2027. As for N3, management announced an additional 3nm fab
expansion in Tainan on strong customer demand, with volume production in 1H28. This
comes on top of 1) the ongoing N3 fab expansion in Tainan (ramp in 1H27), 2) Arizona
fab 2 (ramp in 3Q27, slightly ahead of its prior schedule), 3) Japan fab 2 (ramp in 2028),
and 4) continued N5-to-N3 tool conversion. Beyond Arizona, TSMC confirmed it is
evaluating Texas as a potential additional US site, and is in discussion with one customer

building Terafab on utilizing that capacity; while the business model and structure
remains under discussion, TSMC highlighted that its principle is to run the fab by itself.
GM: steeper N2 dilution near term, but “no ceiling” long term
3Q26 GM declined 1.3ppt QoQ to 66.4%, primarily on dilution from the initial N2 ramp,
and 4Q26 GM is guided down a further 40bps to 66.0% at the mid-point on continued
N2 dilution and overseas fabs. Management now expects overseas fab dilution to be

closer to 2% for full-year 2026 (vs. 2-3% previously), helped by larger scale and cost
improvement, but reiterated it will widen to 3-4% in the latter part of the five-year
period as more US fabs ramp. Longer term, management stated there is “no ceiling” for
TSMC’s profitability, though any dramatic increase will not happen overnight. On pricing,
management reiterated a strategic approach by segment, location and technology, with
US production priced differently from Taiwan. Overall, we slightly lower our GM to

66.6%/66.8%/67.7% in 2026-2028E (vs. 67.2%/67.5%/67.8% previously).
CapEx: 2026 unchanged, multi-year step-up reiterated
2026 CapEx guidance was unchanged at US$60-64bn. While management did not
provide numeric guidance beyond 2026, but reiterated that 2026-2028 CapEx will be
significantly higher than the prior three years, and that revenue growth should continue
to outpace CapEx growth. Management acknowledged the tools, material and labor
inflation, but said the majority of CapEx is driven by long-term demand. Net net, we

maintain our 2026E/2027E/2028E CapEx at US$64bn/US$85bn/US$98bn.
High confidence in its structural lead
Addressing questions regarding rising competition, management remains confident in its
technology lead  for both front end wafer and advanced packaging technology. Despite
the later adoption timeframe (planned for in 2030) vs peers on High NA EUV, TSMC does
not see the later adoption than peers as a disadvantage. In advanced packaging, TSMC is
in volume production at 5x reticle size, which management believes no peer can match

today, with 9x and 14x in development and CoPoS for future cost reduction. In addition,
management also highlighted that it expects A14 (volume production in 2028) to be a
bigger node than N2, with customer engagement already exceeding N2’s at the same
stage.

### Page 5

3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance recap

4Q26 guidance: revenue well above expectations, GM below GSe 
For 4Q26, management guided revenue to US$52.3-54.3bn (+14% QoQ at the 
mid-point; based on FX [TWD/USD] of 31.8), GM to 65-67% and OpM to 56.5-58.5%. 
Revenue guidance came in well above GSe of +11.0% QoQ, supported by continued 
strong leading-edge demand including the steep N2 ramp. However, the GM mid-point 
of 66.0% is 1.3ppt below GSe of 67.3% and broadly in line with consensus of 66.1%, as 
continued N2 dilution and overseas fabs are only partly offset by productivity gains and 
cross-node capacity optimization. 
3Q26 results: revenue and EPS beat, GM below GSe 
3Q26 revenue of NT$1,494.2bn (US$46.72bn) was up 17.6% QoQ and 50.9% YoY, above 
the high end of guidance in USD terms and above GSe/consensus by 0.8%/2.1%. EPS of 
NT$29.43 was up 8.0% QoQ and 68.7% YoY, above GSe/consensus by 1.2%/3.7%; 
excluding the NT$2.24 one-off gain from the VIS disposal in 2Q26, EPS grew 18% QoQ. 
GM of 66.4% (-1.3ppt QoQ) landed in the upper half of guidance (65-67%) but 1.1ppt 
below GSe, while OpM of 58.7% beat the high end of guidance (56-58%) on stable OpEx 
at 7.8% of revenue. 
By technology, N2 contributed 8% of wafer revenue (vs. 3% in 2Q26) and N3 33% (vs. 
30%), taking advanced nodes (7nm and below) to 79% (vs. 77%). By platform, HPC rose 
22% QoQ to 68% of revenue (vs. 66%), with smartphone +10% QoQ at 21%. Inventory 
days fell 5 days QoQ to 82 days on higher wafer shipments.

### Page 7

Reiterate Buy; 12m TP maintain at NT$3,300 
We maintain our 12m TP at NT$3,300  based on a target P/E of 22x (unchanged, 
benchmarked to 1.0stdv above 5-year avg. forward P/E) applied to our 2027E EPS. 
Accordingly, we maintain our 12 month TP for the ADR at US$660, based on an 
unchanged FX conversion of USD/TWD of 30.0 and unchanged ADR premium of 20%. 
Our 22x target P/E multiple is also benchmarked to TSMC’s trading average of 22x 
during its previous revenue upcycle where its 3-year revenue CAGR (2019-2021) was 
28%, vs. our 3-year revenue CAGR at 38% during 2025-2028E. 
Our TP implies 35% (ADR 54%) upside to the last close. As a result, we reiterate our Buy 
ratings for TSMC/ADR. We maintain our constructive view on TSMC’s long-term growth 
outlook as we believe the company’s solid technology leadership and execution better 
positions it vs. peers to capture the industry’s long-term structural growth 
opportunities, especially in AI.

### Page 9

Investment Thesis, Price Target Risks and Methodology

Investment Thesis - TSMC (2330.TW/TSM) 
TSMC is a leading global foundry company specializing in cutting-edge nodes. Its leading 
technology stance enables it to enjoy more than 60% revenue share in the global 
foundry market. We like TSMC as we believe its solid technology leadership and 
execution better position it vs. peers to capture the industry’s long-term structural 
growth opportunities, particularly in areas such as AI/5G/HPC/EV. In addition, valuation 
looks attractive in our view, with the shares trading at the mid-range of their 10-year 
trading history (P/E: 10-29x). Furthermore, we view TSMC as the key AI enabler among 
our Taiwan Semis coverage, thanks to its leadership stance in leading edge nodes and 
advanced packaging technology – CoWoS (chip on wafer on substrate). We believe TSMC 
will achieve its 25% revenue CAGR target for the next several years, driven primarily by 
increasing silicon content growth and AI/HPC demand, with LT GM to remain at 56%+. 
We are Buy rated.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - TSMC (2330.TW/TSM) 
Valuation methodology: We have a 12m TP of NT$3,300, which is derived by applying a 
target P/E multiple of 22x to our 2027E EPS. Our 22x target P/E is benchmarked against 
1.0stdv above its 5-year trading average. For the ADR (TSM), we have a 12m TP of 
US$660, based on a USD/TWD rate of 30.0 and an ADR premium of 20%. 
Key downside risks to our views: (1) further deterioration in end-demand recovery 
impacting capacity utilization; (2) slower customer node migrations; (3) slowdown in AI 
investment resulting in lower long-term semiconductor content growth; (4) poor 
yields/execution resulting in worse-than-expected profitability; (5) stronger 
competition resulting in ASP/profitability erosion; and (6) unfavorable FX trend or 
higher-than-expected cost increase weighing on the margin outlook.
15 October 2026   9

動態更新 IC製造 ‧ 台灣

台積電 
(2330.TW /2330 TT)

多、更多、再更多的 N3 產能擴張 
重要訊息 
台積電 3Q26 財報表現符合預期，管理層預估 2026 年營收將年增 44%。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 符合預期，4Q26 展望強勁。3Q26 毛利率 66.4%，受 N2 量產爬坡稀
釋影響，較我們預估低 1.6 個百分點，但符合公司財測。受惠營收成長及營
業費用控管，營業利益及營益率均符合預期。台積電 4Q26 營收財測為 523
億至 543 億美元，以中間值計算季增 14%，較我們先前預估高出 7%，毛
利率及營益率則預期持平，主要受惠 HPC 需求強勁及 N2 量產爬坡。 
N3 持續擴產，並評估美國亞利桑那州以外的擴產機會 。隨 4Q26 展望轉
強，台積電將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先 40%以上上調至 44%，並
維持全年資本支出 600 億至 640 億美元。台積電過去鮮少將單一製程產能
擴充至超過原先規劃的目標( 約每月 15 萬片)。然而，除近期宣布的三座晶圓
廠(台南 Fab 18 、亞利桑那州二廠及熊本二廠) 外，台積電再宣布於台南新增
一座 N3 晶圓廠，預計於 1H28 量產。整體而言，我們預估 N3 產能將由
2026 年底的每月 15 萬至 16 萬片，在未來兩年進一步增加 8 萬片，增幅達
50%。我們認為此次擴產反映三項需求：(1)Agentic AI 帶動的 CPU 需求；
(2)HBM4e 客製化基底晶粒；以及(3)AI ASIC 。此外，台積電亦正評估在美國
亞利桑那州以外地區擴產的可能性，例如德州，但目前尚未做出最終決定。  
不會將任何商機拱手讓給競爭對手 。管理層多次強調不會讓競爭對手取得任
何商機，體現在 N3 產能的積極擴充。此外，儘管我們預期 Google(美 )委託
Intel(美 )生產的 Humufish 目前沒有備援計劃。然而，在必要情況下，台積電
將協助客戶確保產品如期交付， 暗示若 Intel 的 EMIB-T 製程進度落後，台積
電有望進一步提升市占率。部分投資人可能擔憂台積電導入 ASML High -NA
設備的時程較競爭對手晚兩年，進而影響其技術領先地位。但我們持不同看
法，採用最新設備並不必然代表技術領先。若 在 2030 年前台積電能在不投
資昂貴設備的情況下達到相近的製程效能，反而 有望取得相較同業更顯著的
成本優勢。 
投資建議  
儘管 3Q26 財報符合預期，且我們預估 4Q26 表現將更為強勁，但在市場預
期極高下，投資人仍可能對短期毛利率走勢有所疑慮。考量未來的成長動能
及穩健的執行能力，我們建議投資人趁股價回檔時逢低布局。我們僅微幅調
整獲利預估， 但隨評價基準遞延至 2028 年，我們 將目標價上調至 3,650
元，仍基於景氣上行週期平均的 20 倍本益比，維持「增加持股」評等 。 
投資風險  
全球景氣不確定性；積極擴產導致供給過剩。

法說會重點數據 
 4Q26 財測： 營收預估為 523 億至 543 億美元，以中間值計算季增
14%；毛利率 65-67%，營益率 56.5- 58.5%。 
 2026 年展望：全年營收成長預估上調至年增 44%，維持資本支出 600
億至 640 億美元。  
 毛利率稀釋：海外擴產預計稀釋 2026 年毛利率 2 個百分點，未來幾年
稀釋幅度將擴大至 3-4 個百分點。  
 產能規劃：  
 N2 製程家族產能預計於 2027 年增加至兩倍以上。  
 台南新增 N3 晶圓廠預計於 1H27 量產，另一座 N3 晶圓廠則規劃於
1H28 量產。 
 亞利桑那州 N3 二廠已完成廠房建設，目前正進行設備搬入及安
裝，預計於 3Q27 量產，略早於原訂時程。  
 日本 N3 製程預計於 2028 年量產。 
 A14 製程已開始進行產能準備，預計於 2027 年如期試產，並於
2028 年量產。

台灣 台積電

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 6

ESG 圖表定義  
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力(公用事業為出售而生產) 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者(太陽能、風能、水能、生
物質能、地熱能)之量 
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2約當排放量 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

CO2約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸)銷售(百萬元)比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因(自願或非自願)離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 = (離開之員工/平均員工人數)*100 
- 平均員工人數 = (本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數)/2 
- 本財年末員工數 = 上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練(如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展) 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1天 = 8小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv，凱基

### Page 1

기업분석 2025.09.28 www.daishin.com

Issue & News 
SK하이닉스  
(000660) 
 소음보다 방향성에 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 3,200,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.09.23) 
1,862,000 
반도체업종

◼ 최근 시장은 2가지 Risk를 제기. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 
◼ 현 주가는 보다 강인해지는 Fundamental의 가치를 미반영한 수준. 
◼ 비중 확대 전략 유효. 목표주가와 매수의견 유지.

투자전략: 업황을 대변하지 못하고 있는 주가 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 현 주가는 업황의 강세를 충분히 반영하지 못하
고 있다고 생각. 1) 메모리반도체 사이클의 추가 확장 (구매 경쟁 심화에 따른 
단기 가격 상승 폭 확대, 기대치를 상회할 4Q26 DRAM 판매량), 2) 3Q26 실
적발표에서 보다 구체화될 주주환원 정책의 방향성에 보다 집중할 필요. 
시장에서 제기하는 Risk가 있다면, 2가지. 1) 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈, 
2) Solidigm IPO와 해외 공장 투자에 따른 경계심리 확대가 그 대상. 당사는 
해당 Risk가 Fundamental을 저해할 것으로 보고 있지 않으며, 단기 노이즈보
다는 사이클 확장의 선순환에 보다 주목할 필요가 있다고 생각. 
1. HBM4 품질 이슈, 과도한 우려는 기우 
3Q26부터 HBM4 출하가 본격화될 것이라는 기존 입장을 유지. HBM4 비중
은 3Q26 전체 판매량의 40%, 2027년 연간 50% 중반까지 상승할 것. 
일각에서는 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈로 사업 성과가 기대치를 하회할 
수 있다는 우려를 표출. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 후공정 단에서 문제가 일
부 발생했을 수 있으나, 해결 불가능한 Risk라 보기 어렵고, 불량에 따른 배
상금을 논할 단계도 아니라는 판단. 
2. 해외 공장 투자, 실보다 득이 크다 
언론보도에 따르면, SK하이닉스는 해외 공장 투자와 Solidigm IPO를 추진 중. 
해당 결정이 현실화될 경우, 시장은 단기적으로 이를 부정적으로 받아들일 
가능성이 존재. 다만, 결과적으로, 실보다 득이 큰 선택이 될 것이라 생각. 
- 해외 투자가 현실화되더라도, 실질 생산 기여 시점은 2028년 이후일 가능
성이 높음. 미국의 건설 경기와 공장 구축의 세부 단계 등을 감안할 필요. 
- 해외 투자 결정이 관세 Risk를 축소시킬 수 있는 변화라는 점도 감안할 필
요. “불확실성 해소”는 Upcycle의 중반부에서 반도체 주식에 대한 좋은 투자
포인트가 되어왔고, 이번에도 다르지 않을 것이라는 판단.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%) 과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%) 을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 7

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

Editorial 
A Fed credibility-induced bounce  
Strategy summary:  The Fed’s unanimous decision to hike 
rates this week for the first time since 2023 , and pencil in 
another hike by December, confirms our expectations – flagged 
in the last two weeklies - of a central bank willing to establish its 
independence against political pressure. The Fed’s moderately 
hawkish pivot is helping restore its inflation-fighting credentials. 
The BoJ also hiked rates this week. It is the first time on record 
all three major central banks – Fed, ECB and BoJ – have hiked 
in the same month , as they respond primarily to a resurgence 
in energy-driven inflation amid tight job markets.  
We expect the Fed to hike once more by year-end to 4.0-4.25% 
and follow up with another hike in H1 2027 to 4.25 -4.5%. With 
the revised estimates, we revise up our US government bond 
yield forecasts. Nevertheless, with Fed policy uncertainty 
easing, we see an opportunity to use the equity market dip to 
add exposure amid solid corporate earnings.  
Fed moves to restore credibility:  The Fed’s policy pivot this 
week followed Chair Warsh’s hawkish Jackson Hole speech 
and recent economic data prints, namely: a pick -up in job 
creation and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in 
oil prices due to the re -escalation of the Middle E ast conflict. 
Given the unanimous Fed decision to hike rates this week, we 
expect two more hikes by  June 2027 on the back of  robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated. We expect this pivot to help the Fed 
rebuild credibility as an inflation fighter.  
Policy outlook less aggressive than market pricing : The 
updated Fed policy rate estimates are lower compared with 
money market pricing of three more rate hikes by June 2027 
since we expect disinflation to take hold by Q2 2027 as the 
impact of oil prices and tariffs fade. We believe policy rates can 
return to the 4.0–4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility 
of cuts in H2 2027 as inflation pressures ease.  
Upgrading near-term US rates and USD estimates : Based 
on these revised estimates, we also upgrade our 3-month target 
for the US 10 -year government bond yield to 5.0 -5.25%. We 
also revise up our 12 -month target for the US 10 -year bond 
yield to 4.75-5.0% (see page 4).  
Buying the equity dip as focus turns to fundamentals: Fed 
hikes typically do not hurt equities over the medium-term when 
accompanied by strong earnings growth. We estimate a 25bps 
rise in Fed rates (the discount rate) would impact the S&P500 
index and global equities by 3 -4%. However, we expect the 
headwind from higher rates to be offset by strong earnings 
growth, driving positive performance for global equities.  
Furthermore, a hawkish Fed is largely priced in and we expect 
the Fed to deliver fewer rate hikes than currently priced by 
markets. Meanwhile, we believe concerns over AI industry 
leaders slowing the pace of development are overdone. Hence, 
we maintain our preference for a diversified group of large cap 
technology and semiconductor industry leaders (see page 5-6). 
BoJ hikes; modestly bullish JPY : The BoJ hiked rates by 
25bps to a three-decade high of 1.25%, as expected, but the 7-
2 split vote led to renewed JPY weakness on concerns it may 
slow the pace of further hikes. We remain constructive on JPY 
amid narrowing rate differential vs peers as the BoJ potentially 
hikes rates by 25bps once per quarter until Q2 2027 to curb 
rising domestic wage-driven inflation pressure . Any sign of BoJ 
slowing the pace of hikes could lead to renewed JPY weakness, 
fuelling inflationary pressures (see page 7).  
BoE holds; rangebound GBP: The BoE held rates this week, 
bucking the hiking trend among peers, as weakening UK job 
and housing markets offset rebound in energy-driven inflation. 
We expect BoE to deliver fewer rate hikes than the 100bps of 
increase currently priced by markets  as the job market slows 
further. This is likely to keep GBP rangebound (see page 7).  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions 
  Where do you see US government bond  yields following 
the Fed’s 16 September decision to hike interest rates? 
Our view: The US 10-year government bond yield is likely to 
retest the 5.00-5.25% range in the near term. We favour 3-7-year 
maturity bond s in t he US, preferring credit over government 
bonds. We are looking for an opportunity to extend duration 
approaching Q2 2027. 
Rationale: Over the next few months, we expect 2- and 30-year US 
government bond yields to remain rangebound, but the 10-year yield 
is expected to rebound up to the 5.00-5.25% range. The reasons for 
this include: 1) oil prices staying elevated and potentially rise further 
due to the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle East, with Saudi 
Arabia’s East-West oil pipeline shut, the Houthis now firmly in control 
of the Strait of Bab-al-Mandab, the Strait of Hormuz still closed and 
China returning as a buyer of crude oil in the market ; 2) despite 
notable AI leaders expressing the need to slow AI development, AI 
capex plans are showing no signs of slowing down, which continues 
to drive above-trend growth in the economy. This, in turn, also means 
AI-related heavy bond issuance  is expected to continue. These 
issuers typically issue long-dated tenors, and this competition for 
capital will also exert upward pressure on the US 10-year yields. 
We note that the US 10-year bond yield has room to move lower, 
driven by short-covering activities in the very near term as net-short 
positions had moved to extreme levels before th is week’s  Fed 
meeting. However, we think such downshifts would be short-lived 
and the central tendency for the 10-year yield would be to move back 
up to the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Meanwhile, we see US 30-year yields continuing to reflect the dire 
fiscal outlook for the US government. We expect US 2-year yields 
to continue trading in a range that reflects expectations of further 
federal funds rate hikes. For the record, we only expect two more 
rate hikes from the Fed into the middle of 2027 , while money 
markets are pricing in three more for the same period. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect oil prices to moderate (either 
through a resolution of the M iddle East conflict or a re-routing of oil 
supply chains)  and the inflationary effects from tariffs to fade. 
Inflation by then is likely be close to – if not at – the Fed’s target of 
2%. At this point, we see room for the Fed to contemplate rate 
cuts. US 2-year yields (the tenor that is most sensitive to Fed policy 
changes) will likely start to price th is in. With oil prices and inflation 
cooling, 10-year yields would also have room to come down. 
US 30-year yields, on the other hand, are expected to  remain 
elevated as the US fiscal deficit remains substantial next year and 
could become much worse than its already dire state today. As a 
result, the ‘term premium ’ may keep the ultra -long end elevated. 
Short of some unorthodox policy to artificially cap them lower, 30 -
year yields could continue to drift higher into H2 2027. 
We continue to favour the 3-7-year segment of the yield curve and 
prefer credit over government bond s. We see an opportunity for 
us to extend duration closer to Q2 2027. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO for Fixed Income and FX

for US government bond yields

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Will the step -up in the  Fed’s risk-free rate trigger a re -
pricing of equities? 
Our view: We expect strong corporate earnings growth to offset 
the headwind of higher rates , driving positive performance for 
global equities. We remain Overweight global equities. 
Rationale: The Fed’s latest 25bps rate hike had been anticipated 
by the bond market, partly reflected in the bond yield  rise we have 
seen so far in 2026. Using the US 10-year government bond yield as 
a proxy for the risk -free rate, the yield has risen by 80-85bps this 
year. We estimate that each 25bps rise in the discount rate presents 
a valuation drag on global equities. On this basis, the rise in yields 
represents a potential 10-14% headwind for equities this year. 
However, the Fed’s hike reflects a solid expansion of US economic 
activity, resilient domestic spending and robust capital investment. 
This is consistent with the positive earnings revisions we have seen 
this year, with  2026 earnings growth projections now at 34% (and 
16% for 2027). Global equities are up 14% year-to-date (YTD), but 
even with a 14% valuation headwind from higher yields, we believe 
they can rise further, given strong earnings growth in 2026 and 2027. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is the outlook for the US 10-year government bond 
yield? What is your tactical view on the DXY? 
Our view: We raise our three-month US 10 -year government 
bond yield forecast to the 5.00-5.25% range. Meanwhile, we see 
a near-term upward bias in the DXY after its recent downtrend, 
supported by recent Fed tightening and higher  short-term US 
bond yields.  
Rationale: The Fed 's decision  to raise the target range for the 
federal funds rate by 25bps to 3.75-4.00% validates the upside policy 
risks we flagged recently, following Fed Chair Warsh's Jackson Hole 
speech and a run of firmer US economic data. We expect growth 
to remain robust, the labour market  to remain tight and inflation to 
stay elevated, supporting one more hike before year-end 2026 and 
potentially another in H1 2027  – fewer than the three additional 
hikes currently priced in by money markets . For the US 10-year 
government bond yield, further policy tightening and lingering US 
fiscal concerns should keep yields elevated, but the Fed’s visible 
response to inflation should limit additional upward pressure, leaving 
the 10-year yield holding around the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Crucially, Warsh provided no forward guidance on further rate 
hikes. Markets had already priced in substantial additional rate hikes 
ahead of the Fed meeting, reducing the scope for further USD upside 
purely from rate repricing. Meanwhile, we expect further policy 
tightening from the ECB and the BoJ, while the RBA also remains 
biased towards further tightening. This should gradually narrow the 
US rate advantage and limit the extent of a DXY rebound. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Anthony Naab, CFA, Investment Strategist

Global equities’ earnings growth estimates have 
been revised higher this year, offsetting the 
headwind from higher rates

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in technology as AI lab 
CEOs call for a slowdown in frontier AI model development? 
Our view: We believe concerns over “pacing the frontier” are 
overdone. We maintain our preference to remain invested in 
and diversified across Big Tech and semiconductor leaders. 
Rationale: We believe it is prudent for investors to avoid 
overreacting to the recent calls for a slowdown in frontier AI model 
development, as they focus on responsible AI development, rather 
than halting AI model development or reducing spending. The global 
AI leadership  race, reinforced by the US and China’s  strategic 
ambitions, also makes a coordinated slowdown unlikely. 
While the near-term narrative may shift from AI training beneficiaries 
towards inference plays, hyperscalers continue to signal strong AI 
infrastructure commitments and improving monetisation, supporting 
our AI capex outlook. Rising AI adoption and agentic use cases 
should sustain inference demand, even if training activity moderates, 
benefiting custom AI chips, NAND flash memory and hyperscalers.  
We believe the decline in valuations  this year more than 
compensates for any downside risk to AI capex. Our Opportunistic 
idea on Global Semiconductors further reflects our conviction, with 
attractive valuations supporting selective additions during volatility. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  With ASEAN and UK equities outperforming AI-focused 
markets in H2 2026, is it worth adding to these markets? 
Our view: ASEAN and UK equities offer portfolio diversification, 
but we are Underweight both markets in favour of US and Asia 
ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, given their stronger earnings potential. 
Rationale: The ASEAN and UK markets have outperformed the 
MSCI ACWI over the past three months, but both have 
underperformed on a YTD basis. Financials is the largest sector in 
the ASEAN (53%) and the UK (28%) and provides steady earnings. 
However, both markets have minimal exposure to the technology 
sector (just 3% and 1% , respectively), ultimately dragging down 
growth. Hence, while defensive markets offer value by increasing 
diversification and reducing volatility in a portfolio, we maintain our 
Underweight allocation for ASEAN and the UK, as we see much 
stronger earnings potential elsewhere in the US and AxJ markets.  
We remain Overweight US and AxJ, supported by robust earnings 
growth, as  AI still has the potential to become a major economic 
growth driver . Our 12-month S&P500 Index forecast of 8,400  
implies low-teens upside, supported by a strong earnings backdrop 
of 25-30% earnings-per-share growth for 2026 and 15 -20% for 
2027. AxJ also boasts the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, fuelled by the AI semiconductor super-cycle. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is it still appropriate to have a positive stance on US 
high-yield (HY) bonds? 
Our view: We remain opportunistically bullish on US HY bonds. 
Lower sensitivity to interest rate volatility, a below-trend default 
rate and a maturity wall pushed out to 2028-29 drive this view. 
Rationale: Recent interest rate volatility has highlighted a key 
structural advantage of US HY bonds: their higher coupon rates  
and shorter tenors relative to Developed Market (DM) government 
bonds and US investment-grade (IG) corporate bonds translate into 
a much lower sensitivity to interest rates, cushioning bond prices 
during yield backup episodes, where bond yields rise sharply. 
YTD, US HY has delivered 1.8% in total returns, outperforming DM 
government bonds and US IG corporate bonds by over 3% each on 
a relative basis. This outperformance was particularly evident during 
the recent back-up in bond yields, as inflation concerns re-emerged 
after renewed Middle East tensions – an environment where HY's 
lower rate sensitivity provided a relative cushion  even as broader 
fixed income markets came under pressure in absolute terms. On 
the fundamentals side, US HY corporate credit quality remains 
resilient. Default rates continue to track below long-run trend levels, 
and the maturity wall has been pushed out to 2028 -29, reducing 
near-term refinancing risk. Against a non -recessionary macro 
backdrop, we expect these credit fundamentals to hold, supporting 
the case for continued opportunistic exposure to the asset class.  
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Do the latest BoJ and BoE meetings alter your view on the 
central banks’ rate paths and their corresponding currencies? 
Our view: We remain constructive on the JPY and expect two 
BoJ rate hikes this year and potentially one hike per quarter in 
H1 2027 as policy normalisation continues. Meanwhile, the BoE 
held rates at 3.75%. We expect GBP/USD to stay rangebound 
within 1.32-1.36 over the next 1-3 months, with downside risk 
towards the lower end of our medium-term 1.30-1.36 range. 
Rationale: Persistent inflation, higher energy prices and earlier JPY 
weakness support further BoJ policy normalisation . Continued 
hiking should progressively narrow the US -Japan rate gap, reduce 
the attractiveness of JPY -funded carry trades and support  the JPY 
over the medium term towards the 153-155 range. However, with a 
September hike largely priced  in, future tightening ’s pace  will be 
more important for the JPY. Meanwhile, the BoE was split 6-3 in its 
decision to hold rates at 3.75% , with three members preferring a 
25bps hike, signalling elevated inflation risks. However, a weaker 
labour market is likely to limit the pace of BoE tightening. The US-
UK rate divergence is expected to favour the USD over the next 1-3 
months. Further BoE tightening (if inflation remains elevated ) could 
narrow the rate gap and provide support to the GBP later in the cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist

We expect US HY bonds to continue delivering 
strong performance in the near term

Document 220: Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
History tends to repeat itself in advanced economies. Once again, growth ultimately fell short of expectations by only a small
margin in the first half of 2026, despite the conflict in Iran. As early as 2025, the impact of tariffs was less severe than feared.
This is a sign that structural factors (notably AI, defence and electrification) are underpinning growth in these countries

and are expected to continue to do so in 2027. However, there are significant risks to the economic outlook, primarily of a
geopolitical nature, with a significant upside risk to inflation and interest rates. The other instance of history repeating itself
concerns a period that those under 20 cannot have experienced: the period leading up to the 2008 crisis, echoes of which can
be seen in terms of growth, inflation, the direction of monetary policy, and long-term interest rates.

Advanced economies are experiencing trends similar to those they
faced nearly 20 years ago:
- Growth remains resilient in 2025-26 despite shocks, as it did in 2006-
07, in the major advanced economies.
- Inflation is above central banks’ targets: as in 2006-07, this calls for a
tightening of monetary policy.
- In Europe and the United States, long-term interest rates have re -
turned to their 2006-07 levels (1996 in Japan): a challenging environ-

ment for public finances in a context where public debt ratios are signi-
ficantly higher than those seen 20 years ago.
THE GLOBAL ECONOMY IN THE MIDST OF A GROWTH CYCLE
We are already well into 2026, and the picture so far is clear: glo -
bal growth is proving resilient. Following a US tariff shock last year,
growth is once again proving, for the time being, to be significantly less
affected by the conflict in Iran than had been anticipated when it first

broke out. Initially, however, there were fears that this new shock would
hit growth even harder. As with the tariff hike in 2025, the impact was
measurable, particularly in terms of consumer confidence. But in both
2025 and 2026, growth has also shown a degree of resilience.
The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise
in oil prices, the recessionary impact of which is not yet apparent.
According to our forecasts, growth was expected to reach or exceed 1%

in 2026 in the four major advanced economies (the Eurozone, Japan,
the United Kingdom and the United States, where it exceeds 2%) for
the second consecutive year. This had not happened (excluding the
post-COVID catch-up period) since 2006-07. Growth figures are more
moderate (particularly for demographic reasons), but the private sec -
tor is not (at present) showing the same excesses (at that time, the
credit boom had preceded the worst crisis since the 1930s); although

debt levels have recently risen in the US tech sector.
The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial
intelligence (AI). It is fuelling global trade and business investment
in most countries. This is clearly the case in the United States, where
business investment is growing strongly as investment plans in the
US tech sector are implemented.
However, the world’s major economies are currently engaged in a
broader push to expand their capital goods investment. Alongside the

ongoing development of services—particularly digital services—we
have seen a rebound in industrial production in most countries since
early 2025. The leading sectors are primarily technology equipment,
machinery, aeronautics, defence and their essential inputs (energy and
metals). This trend is evident everywhere, including in Europe, and
is becoming entrenched over the long term, with an improvement in
business climate indicators, particularly new export orders. Growth is

therefore expected to strengthen further in 2027, particularly in the
Eurozone, where German investment plans are set to gather momen -
tum and whose spillover effects will have a greater impact on the eco-
nomy.
However, not all sectors are experiencing the same momentum. This
is particularly true for the sectors that depend on household consump-
tion, which has shown mixed trends. Our observation that consumer
spending is more buoyant in the United States than in Europe was

confirmed in 2026, and the outlook does not point to a change in this
ranking.
EDITORIAL
RESILIENCE TO SHOCK AFTER SHOCK

### Page 2

4
EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
While European and US households are experiencing more or less the
same energy price shock, inflation remains higher in the United States.
But this has not prevented consumer spending from keeping pace, pri-
marily because it is driven by the wealthiest households, who are less
sensitive to inflation than to the wealth effect. Furthermore, while the

savings rate remains high in the Eurozone, US households have been
drawing down their savings. This is also true of British households,
which explains why the UK has seen slightly stronger growth since
the beginning of the year. In Japan, government spending has partially
offset energy inflation (while other countries are doing so to a much
lesser extent than in 2022), which has supported consumption growth.
However, these buffers—reduced savings or fiscal support—have their

limits when shocks persist. The two major current conflicts (Iran and
Ukraine) are far from being resolved, and their impact is continuing.
This persistent geopolitical risk and its implications for inflation repre-
sent a significant headwind to the growth outlook for 2027.
HIGHER INFLATION CALLS FOR MONETARY TIGHTENING
Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks
examined in this publication, which is another similarity (albeit with

different underlying causes) with the 2006-07 period. Japan is the ex -
ception, as government support measures for households are tempo -
rarily holding back inflation.
Apart from the Bank of Japan, which is engaged in a long-term norma-
lisation process, no central bank was expected to raise its key rates
in 2026. But they are now being prompted to do so for reasons that
vary from one region to another. The first is the conflict in Iran and
the resulting rise in oil prices, which triggered a rebound in energy

inflation from the second quarter onwards. However, since the conflict
was not resolved by the memorandum of understanding signed in June,
and given that the subsequent drop in oil prices proved short-lived,
inflation is expected to rise again. The rise in gas prices is expected to
exacerbate the impact, particularly in Europe, where inventories are
being replenished ahead of winter. Electricity prices are expected to
follow suit in most countries, but at a more moderate pace than in

2022. Indeed, gas is now used less to generate electricity than it was
at that time (thanks to the expansion of renewable energy), and to an
even lesser extent in France.
There is a significant risk that energy inflation will spread further to
other components of inflation in the future, and inflation expectations
could eventually reflect this. This is why the European Central Bank
has already raised its key interest rate twice (in June and September)

and, according to our scenario, will likely do so again in December. For
the same reason, we expect the Bank of England to raise its policy rate
in November. Although the cap on energy prices was maintained for
a long time, it has now been raised. As a result, inflation is expected
to rise between now and the end of the year. The aim will then be to
prevent second-round effects.
Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength. In the

United States, it has exceeded the 2% target since 2021. The main
argument in favour of a new cycle of rate hikes (following the one in
2022-2023) lies in the current resilience of growth and the improving
labour market. The Federal Reserve (Fed) must balance two equally
important objectives: maximum employment and price stability. Howe-
ver, unlike in the 4 th quarter of 2025, when the decline in job crea -
tion justified monetary easing (by 75 basis points), the current sharp

rebound in job creation completely reverses that analysis. According
to our forecasts, the unemployment rate is expected to decrease in
the coming months, which pushes back the prospect of a decline in
core inflation. This situation persists due to both supply constraints
(over which a central bank has no control) and excess demand (which
the Fed can, however, mitigate). Consequently, the Fed raised its key
rate in September and is expected to implement two further rate hikes

between now and January 2027.
In Japan, finally, while headline inflation has been brought down by
government support measures, core inflation remains significant.
Wage pressures are mounting against a backdrop of labour shortages.
The Bank of Japan is therefore being forced to accelerate the pace of its
monetary normalisation.
In all these regions, inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026
or Q1 2027. It is against this backdrop of temporary inflation that rate

hikes could also come to an end by that time (excluding Japan). Howe-
ver, this assumes that once the energy shock—the course of which
remains uncertain—subsides, inflation will fall. The effects of El Niño,
which are still difficult to gauge at present, could take over. Inflation
would then remain above the central banks’ target for a longer period.
Under this scenario, rate hikes would go further than is currently en -
visaged.
THE RISE IN LONG- TERM INTEREST RATES: A FACTOR THAT WILL WEIGH

ON PUBLIC FINANCES
Monetary policies have also to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal
policy, particularly in the United States (where the budget deficit is
high and shows no signs of shrinking) and in Japan (where the new
government is pursuing pro-growth policies). In Europe, fiscal policy
is playing a much smaller role than it did in 2022. While the stimulus
measures implemented at that time did help reduce the inflation rate
in the short term (by nearly 2.5 pp in France, for example), by keeping

demand at a high level, they delayed disinflation and increased the
scale of the monetary tightening required.
The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: it triggered a
rise in long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the
start of the year) to levels close to those of 2006-07. This rise is wides-
pread, but its causes vary. Inflation expectations appear to play a more
significant role in the Eurozone, whereas the rise in real interest rates

seems to be the main factor in the United States and Japan.

### Page 3

5
EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
Higher inflation and more resilient real growth than feared point in
the same direction: stronger than expected nominal growth that is li -
kely to be sustained. However, in the long term, high nominal growth
tends to drive up nominal interest rates (as it is a positive sign of more
profitable investment projects), in contrast to the problem of secular

stagnation raised in the 2010s (which combined low nominal growth
with low nominal interest rates).
The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period
of growth), with various situations. In several countries, the public
debt-to-GDP ratio continues to rise. In the United States, deficit reduc-
tion does not appear to be a priority, to the extent that it is expected
to remain above 6% of GDP at the very least until the end of Donald

Trump’s term of office (with little hope that the mid-term elections will
significantly alter this trend). In the United Kingdom and France, fiscal
consolidation has begun, but it is being hampered by the simultaneous
increase in certain areas of expenditure (debt servicing, defence and
healthcare, due in particular to an ageing population), which is slowing
the pace of consolidation. In Germany, the implementation of invest -
ment plans is leading to a widening of the deficit. While this does not

pose a sustainability problem, it does result in an increase in annual
debt issuance. In Japan, the budget deficit is kept in check by interest
payments that remain low, as the rise in interest rates is recent and
the average maturity of the debt is high (10 years). However, expansio-
nary fiscal policy could pose further problems in terms of debt dyna -
mics if it were to continue through the end of the decade. In Italy and
Spain, fiscal consolidation has been sufficient to stabilise the public

debt-to-GDP ratio (and even to reduce it in Spain), but the high level of
Italy’s debt service it vulnerable to a rise in interest rates.
In all these countries, the fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it
will be in 2030. Indeed, in most cases, only half of the debt was is -
sued after 2022 (that is, after the end of the zero-interest-rate period).
The debt service has so far only partially risen, except in the United
States, where most of the increase has already occurred due to a shor-

ter average debt maturity (6 years). This means that, for all countries
combined, the rate of GDP growth remains higher than the average
interest rate paid on public debt (or the effective interest rate).
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with
GDP growth. As a result, in most cases, the debt service will have re -
turned to the level as a share of GDP seen in the early 2000s. In order to
stabilise public debt-to-GDP ratios, primary balances will need to have

improved. This will even require the generation of primary surpluses,
particularly in Italy (which is already achieving this), France and the
United Kingdom. This is a crucial shift that governments will need to
introduce as early as the 2027 budget; otherwise, they risk postponing
the stabilisation of their public debt-to-GDP ratio once again.
Article completed on 17 September 2026
Stéphane Colliac
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

Document 231: AI暴跌，苹果新高！5万亿市值超越英伟达，这合理吗？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI暴跌，苹果新高！5万亿市值超越英伟达，这合理吗？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20

### Page 1

当科技股因 AI 恐慌哀鸿遍野，苹果却以 " 轻 AI" 低资本开⽀策略逆势登顶，总市值⾸破 5 万亿
美元，与纳斯达克 100 指数的相关性史⽆前例地转负。折叠屏 iPhone Duo 预订⽕爆、
MacBook 触控新品在即，但 36 倍远期市盈率能否撑住这座估值⾼峰， 10 ⽉ 28 ⽇发布会或将
给出答案。
摘要：
当科技股在 AI 恐慌中哀鸿遍野，苹果却悄然登上历史之巅。
苹果股价在上周市场⼤跌中逆势⾛强，总市值⾸次突破 5 万亿美元，逼近英伟达全球最⾼市值宝
座。与此同时，苹果与纳斯达克 100 指数的两个⽉滚动相关性⾸次转为负值 —— 这在苹果历史上
从未发⽣过。这⼀罕⻅的⾛势背后，折射出市场对 AI ⾼资本开⽀路线的深层质疑，以及资⾦
向 " 轻 AI" 防御性科技资产的⼤规模转移。
苹果的强势表现并⾮孤⽴事件。据彭博报道，苹果将于 10 ⽉ 28 ⽇在洛杉矶举办 "Pro and tell" 发
布会，届时将推出⾸款触控屏 MacBook 。此前，公司已相继推出可折叠⼿机 iPhone Duo 及

HomeView 智能家居中枢，新品周期密集。
然⽽，分析师对苹果的前景看法分歧明显：看多者视其为 AI 时代的防御性赢家，看空者则警告当
前估值已严重透⽀基本⾯。
史⽆前例的市场分化：苹果成科技板块 " 避⻛港 "
苹果与⼤盘的背离程度，已超出历史经验范畴。
分析援引⾼盛及彭博数据，苹果与纳斯达克 100 指数的两个⽉滚动相关性已⾸次转负，与费城半
导体指数（ SOX ）的分化同样显著。更值得关注的是，苹果已从 " 七巨头 " 整体⾛势中脱钩，展现
出其他⼤型科技股所不具备的相对强势。
从估值⻆度看，苹果当前远期⾃由现⾦流收益率约为 3.15% ，⾼于纳斯达克 100 指数整体⽔平，
这使其在机构投资者眼中兼具成⻓性与防御性。The Market Ear 将苹果定性为 " 科技板块内部避
险资产的终极选择 " ，并指出其波动率虽有所上升，但与整个科技板块的恐慌情绪相⽐，压⼒依
然有限。
" 轻 AI" 叙事：资本开⽀克制成最⼤卖点
苹果此轮上涨的核⼼逻辑，在于市场对 AI ⾼投⼊模式的重新审视。

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

Melius 分析师 Ben Reitzes 在接受采访时指出，苹果是 " 市场奖励低资本开⽀、 AI 模型⽆关路线的
最佳范本 " ，并维持买⼊评级，⽬标价 370 美元。相⽐之下， Alphabet 、微软、亚⻢逊等竞争对
⼿均计划向 AI 基础设施投⼊数千亿美元，⽽苹果迄今在这⼀领域的直接投⼊相对克制。
The Market Ear 亦在此前 9 ⽉ 23 ⽇的报告中提出，苹果正成为 AI 推理交易的 " 意外受益者 "—— 其
边缘端 AI 部署策略，或在算⼒需求格局演变中占据独特位置。⾃该报告发布以来，苹果股价表现
已明显跑赢英伟达，六个⽉维度的分化尤为突出。
iPhone Duo 预订⽕爆，但分析师存疑
苹果折叠屏⼿机 iPhone Duo 上周五开启预订，起售价 1999 美元，最⾼配置售价 3199 美元。苹果
官⽅将预订需求形容为 " ⾮同寻常 " ，交货⽇期迅速延伸⾄明年 1 ⽉。
然⽽，分析师对这⼀热度的解读并不⼀致。 Jefferies 分析师 Edison Lee 认为，预订数据显⽰今年

出货量约为 600 万⾄ 700 万部，符合市场预期，并⽆超预期之处。他将交货延迟归因于 " 较低的⽣
产良率 " ，⽉产能上限约为 200 万部，同时指出需求中可能混杂了 " 倒卖套利 " 的投机性购买。 Lee
维持苹果跑输⼤市评级，⽬标价 263.66 美元。
Rosenblatt Securities 分析师 Barton Crockett 同样维持中性评级，⽬标价 303 美元。他在报告中
指出，iPhone Duo 的⽣产受到可折叠 OLED ⾯板及铰链组件良率偏低的制约，来⾃媒体报道显
⽰今年产量在 600 万⾄ 1000 万部之间，与华尔街预期基本吻合。
⾼端化战略：均价提升对冲销量隐忧
苹果今秋未推出标准版 iPhone 18 ，仅提供 Pro 、 Pro Max 及 Duo 三款⾼端机型，⼊⻔级 iPhone
18 预计要到明年春季才会上市。
KeyBanc 分析师 Brandon Nispel 对此持保留态度。他在客⼾报告中援引 iPhone ⽤⼾调查指出，
随着价格上涨、运营商促销收紧，以及现有设备性能已⾜够满⾜⽇常需求，消费者升级意愿正

在下降， " 这最终将导致销量的温和下滑 " 。 Nispel 维持苹果减持评级，⽬标价 250 美元，较上周
五收盘价隐含约 27% 的下⾏空间。
不过，多数分析师认为，⾼端产品组合将推动 iPhone 平均售价显著提升，在⼀定程度上对冲销
量压⼒。美银证券分析师 Wamsi Mohan 维持买⼊评级，⽬标价 370 美元，理由是苹果具备 " 强劲
的资本回报、边缘端 AI 的潜在优势，以及新产品与新市场带来的期权价值 " 。

5 万亿估值：合理溢价还是透⽀未来？
苹果市值突破 5 万亿美元，当前股价对应 2027 财年预期市盈率约为 36 倍，显著⾼于历史均值。
投资者⾯临的核⼼问题在于：苹果的盈利增速能否⽀撑这⼀估值⽔平。今年以来苹果股价已累
计上涨约 26% ，部分投资者对涨势能否持续持怀疑态度。
多空双⽅的分歧，本质上是对苹果商业模式在 AI 时代定位的不同判断：看多⽅认为其 " 轻资产、
重⽣态 " 的路线将在 AI 泡沫破裂后脱颖⽽出；看空⽅则担忧，在缺乏实质性 AI 收⼊催化剂的情况
下，当前估值已难以为继。苹果股价能否守住 5 万亿市值，或将取决于 10 ⽉ 28 ⽇发布会能否带来
真正的惊喜。

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

### Page 5

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

セン
To
◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.
